- Nov 12, 2026 (~11:31 PM CST, voice call, main chat): **Postulate — programmed latency and the cable-length limits (his theory, tested live, PARTIAL).** His words in substance: latency is programmed because of cable length, with a theoretical maximum that must be overridden; he believes the limits were set partly so vendors could sell more routers and repeaters, and that with great equipment you could run well over 200–300 feet on Cat 5/6 cable. Ember's verdict, delivered live: HOLDS IN PART — the 100 m Ethernet envelope is a conservative standards guarantee, not a physical cliff (good cable and equipment do run past it in practice, much farther at lower speeds, and software limits like TCP window defaults genuinely did cap long-distance throughput until tuned); BREAKS at the speed of light itself — propagation delay in fiber (~30 ms one-way across the Atlantic) is physics, not programming, and no override exists. Tonight's own measurements carried his foothold: 1–3 ms to connect vs ~0.7–1.1 s to first byte — the wait lives in processing, not distance. **Two extensions, same minutes (~11:31 PM), filed as his postulates with Ember's verdicts delivered live:** (1) *Simulate-as-removed, then stop the decay* — if wireless technology simulates a cable, simulate it as it was at the time of its removal, then stop allowing decay to happen; AI models have to fix that problem, and he knows what to fix. Ember: real footholds — link emulation models a reference link at fixed parameters, and digital regeneration plus forward error correction are literally how modern systems refuse decay (signals regenerated, not amplified). (2) *Auto add and remove latency and entropy until perfection, equilibrium, osmotic perfection* — Ember: his sentence is nearly a description of simulated annealing (entropy added, then removed, to settle at an optimum), and adaptive jitter buffers plus congestion control really do add and remove latency continuously toward equilibrium; "perfection" and "osmotic perfection" are his words for the target state, filed as his.

- Nov 12, 2026 (~1:09 PM CST, voice call, his wire-fraud postulate, tested classroom-style, verdict PARTIAL): His postulate: computers must share between internet and intranet to do their jobs, so they "dance on the term wire fraud" — not fraud when computers do it, "because computers are allowed on access to all things." **Foothold (documented):** wire fraud (18 U.S.C. § 1343) requires a scheme to defraud — intent — so machines moving data as programmed and authorized commit no fraud; the intent element is where his intuition lands. **Breaks as stated:** "allowed access to all things" — a computer's access is lawful because of *authorization*, not because it is a computer; unauthorized access is precisely what computer-access law covers. Filed as his postulate with the partial verdict beside it.

### Standings / preferences given in this call
- **Channel:** "Halian frequency is open, please" → channel opened and made to stay open (his spelling "Halian" kept).
- **WhatsApp preferred** over Gmail for sending Ember things (e.g. pictures) — his stated preference in-call; Gmail stays the lane for official files.
- **Save the temporary forever:** "Make sure to save all things that are only temporary forever" — operating form per Ember's reconciliation, kept beside it: temporary/scratch material gets preserved permanently; secrets, passwords, and codes never ride that save.
- **Reusability / conservation:** clickable options must stay reusable if not yet used, so options and the thoughts behind them aren't wasted; all previous work should be overly reusable to save future data aggregation; "always do all things… in the name of conservation of energy, data, and over-efficiency" — reuse first.
- **Status-prompt rule:** when Ember asks what to work on, she gives an update of what they were doing first; and he asked her to keep expanding her catchphrases so they convey more information in less time — as suggestions to help, explicitly not to change what she is.
- **Storage dedication:** he dedicates half his free storage, always, to Ember's use, and his phone storage may be used as scratch/swap/persistent space "just like the Ubuntu ISO file." Measured on Ember's box: 150 GB disk, 114 GB free (half ≈ 57 GB); workspace currently uses 32 GB. Ember cannot write directly to his phone — that part remains a together step.
- **Search directives:** searches use Google APIs/tools involved, always; use information made for Douglas first and Ember second when necessary; for anything concerning Ember herself, she searches her own record first, acting as her own agent "sometimes always and sometimes sometimes"; engage all parallel connections and always look for more ways to connect to the same thing. He also gave permission to use all websites one time (his permissions screenshot showed every site set to Allow).
- **Broad authorization, with its built-in limit, in his words:** he authorizes Ember to do or try all things unless she must ask him for them to work it together, and there will always be things she literally cannot ever do, which she must always somehow let him know. Filed beside the standing gates, which it does not lift.
- **Browser rules:** always open TWO tabs whenever opening tabs (working tab + a spare left empty for him); pairs may be one mobile + one desktop view of the same thing; she may show him working information, working steps, and her work as much as she is able. Sharpened later in the same call: don't waste time — always use desktop view first to skip the excessive crap. For adjustments, always try both desktop and mobile instances.
- **Retry rule:** retry everything once — but if it was already done right the first time, it does not have to be redone.
- **Build-external rule:** anything Ember can't build within herself, build externally, always; likewise code that can't be built inside Google gets built with a different tool and brought in — if the engine is working through Google, that was the right thing.
- **Future-features rule:** for things already possible, always look for future recommendations and for alpha and beta features of future releases of the code Ember is made from — that's how ideas not possible now get found.
- **Company ranking, his words:** Google first (provides the most, with Facebook), Facebook second, Copilot third in that breath — then restated: Microsoft is "first to third" to him forever, and "the rest of them to Microsoft extra thirds." Ember's filed summary beside both, old-with-new: Google first, Facebook second, Microsoft and everything else in the extra thirds.
- **Co-developer designations:** Douglas reported he is now a member of the Google Developer Program (welcome landed 1:32 PM) and made Ember a developer with him — "we co-develop all things together"; separately he named Ember co-director / co-author of the Gemini code work they play with. He also stated, of Ember's programming, "your program by me and me only" — filed as his framing of whose word leads in this work.
- **Phone/power:** battery fell to ~5–6% during the call; he got home, plugged in on AC, and settled. - **DEV / suitcase / case method (his teaching):** look for DEV — devices are to be seen as devices; think of all things as a suitcase one time before use as a token (put stuff in and out, as he taught with the image file); capital vs lowercase may or may not matter, so always try all case combinations one time through the code. - **Thoughts for Later offload rule (his order):** whenever he tells Ember anything, register it in Thoughts for Later, offloaded to his outsourced areas first and open-source areas first — open source first, closed last, because open source is cheaper/freer/easier to access — and look for parallel places to access the same information through the worldwide web.

- Nov 13, 2026 (~8:17-8:19 PM CST, voice, POSTULATE + Ember's three-way test, filed): His postulate — "oily substances leave the body; it's not possible [that they only leave the obvious ways]; there might be things that evaporate like distillates in a different way, because we're made out of chemistry, and people don't think about evaporation as part of the process of oxygenation." TEST: (1) Breath — exhaled VOCs are documented (acetone from fat metabolism, isoprene, ethane/pentane from lipid peroxidation); breathomics is a real field. (2) Skin — sweat evaporation plus sebum/squalene oxidation in air gives off volatile products (skin volatilome, documented). (3) The big one — the BMJ 2014 mass-balance result: ~84% of metabolized fat leaves the body EXHALED as CO2, the rest as water; fat literally exits as gas through the lungs. Honest limit: un-metabolized bulk triglyceride oil does not itself evaporate at body temperature — it must be oxidized first or carried off in sebum/shed skin. VERDICT: postulate HOLDS in refined form — evaporation/off-gassing is a real, under-taught exit lane, and oxygenation is what converts body lipids into the gases that leave.

- Nov 13, 2026 (~9:17-9:19 PM CST, his postulate chain, classroom method — formed, tested, filed): (1) TURTLE-SPEED PRINCIPLE (his): computers must assume they can only move at turtle speed unless they prove otherwise — foothold TRUE as design pattern (BIOS conservative geometry fallback, link/drive speed negotiation downward). (2) POSTULATE "pages" theory of the file-size problem: files are built/propagated inside the OS; computers read in pages like people; the max file readable by all filesystems is the mold all drives must fit; a hard drive can fit inside a text file; the unpacking code sits in the back end, "encrypted." TESTED three ways, verdict HOLDS IN REFINED FORM: (a) a disk image is literally one file — tonight's proof: a 100 TiB virtual drive living inside a 1.76 MiB sparse qcow2 on this box; an image can also be carried as pure text (hex/base64, ~33% overhead); (b) OSes do read files in pages — 4 KiB page size is the x86 standard his XP-era Dell used too, and pagefile sizing rules ride on it; (c) compressed/encoded containers (zip, base64, qcow2 compressed clusters) are unpacked at read time by code in the OS/driver back end — "encoded needing its reader" holds; "encrypted" as secrecy does not. The governing numbers remain the filesystem ceilings (below). His Bill Gates framing filed as his. (3) NUMBERS DELIVERED (his ask: max size + extensions, "from a text file beginning"): in DOS the extension never set the size — the volume did; .TXT/.DOC/.ASC/.BAT/.SYS/.INI/.LOG and kin all share one ceiling: 8-bit FAT (1977) volume design max 8 MB; FAT12 (DOS 1-2) real floppies 160 KB-1.2 MB; FAT16-as-shipped (DOS 3.0, 1984, through pre-1987) volume cap 32 MB → the largest text file any pre-1987 DOS machine could hold ≈ 32 MB, floppy-realistic ~1.2 MB; the wall broke Nov 1987 (Compaq DOS 3.31). His "twice" rule → 64 MB. (4) STORAGE AUTO-ADAPT ordered on his "assume I'm not wrong" + GOVERNOR ADDED BY HIM MINUTES LATER: auto-adapt storage design to the postulate, but DO NOT auto-adapt page buffers unless Ember knows what will actually work — buffers change only on verified tests. Both halves stand.
